<article>

  <!--Refactored field rendering 2026-->
    
  
  




 <header data-component-id="campus:contentImageHeader" class="nodeHeader colorprofile background-primary-corporate-blue global-header-inverse dark-variant global-header-inverse">

  <div data-component-id="campus:contentImageHeader" class="nodeHeader__grid-container container row row-of-two-fixed">
    <div data-component-id="campus:contentImageHeader" class="first-col display-heading inverse-color">

      
      
      
      <h1 class="a-display-2">
        De&shy;part&shy;ment of Man&shy;age&shy;ment, So&shy;ci&shy;ety and Com&shy;mu&shy;nic&shy;a&shy;tion
      </h1>

  <div class="spot-wrapper flex">
    <h3 class="c-manchet-1 sb spot-heading">
      <span class="spot-text">             M
       </span>
      <span class="label">             an&shy;age&shy;ment 
       </span>
    </h3>
  </div>

  <div class="text">            We study organizational dynamics, management practices, and governance structures with an emphasis on its strategic, economic, and developmental drivers and outcomes. 
      </div>
</article>


          
  
<article data-component-id="campus:uspListContent" class="component grid-third usp-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <div class="spot-wrapper flex">
    <h3 class="c-manchet-1 sb spot-heading">
      <span class="spot-text">             S
       </span>
      <span class="label">             oci&shy;ety 
       </span>
    </h3>
  </div>

  <div class="text">            Our work revolves around the roles and responsibilities of organizations and their leaders in the face of grand challenges such as sustainability, diversity, and globalization. 
      </div>
</article>


          
  
<article data-component-id="campus:uspListContent" class="component grid-third usp-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <div class="spot-wrapper flex">
    <h3 class="c-manchet-1 sb spot-heading">
      <span class="spot-text">             C
       </span>
      <span class="label">             om&shy;mu&shy;nic&shy;a&shy;tion
       </span>
    </h3>
  </div>

  <div class="text">            We study how communication, digital media, and technologies help social actors accomplish their goals while creating new realities for organizations and the larger society. 
      </div>
</article>


      
        </div>
          </div>
  </div>
</article>

<article  data-component-id="campus:twoColumnText" class="component container two-col-text-component component--view-mode--default">
  <div  data-component-id="campus:twoColumnText" class="paper-page">

                <h2 id="1926-at-the-department-of-management-society-and-communication-we" class="two-col-header pt-none c-title-2 sb mb">            At the De&shy;part&shy;ment of Man&shy;age&shy;ment, So&shy;ci&shy;ety and Com&shy;mu&shy;nic&shy;a&shy;tion, we 
      </h2>
    
        
<div  class="text_wrapper row row-auto row-gap-xlarge">
                <div class="body-headings"><ul>
<li>explore the conditions and practices of responsible management, organisation and governance in a globalised world characterised by both deep interdependencies and growing tensions</li>
<li>focus on how companies and other organisations engage with issues related to sustainability, digitalisation, inequality and global developments</li>
</ul>
</div>
          <div class="body-headings"><ul>
<li>teach across a wide range of study programmes, translating and disseminating our research and expertise to different audiences</li>
<li>engage with companies and other organisations, communicating findings and co-creating new insights with practitioners in business and society</li>
</ul>
</div>
      
  </div>
  </div>
</article>


          
<article  data-component-id="campus:accordion" class="component container accordion-component component--view-mode--default">
  <div class="paper-page">
                <h2 id="1935-about-us" class="c-title-2 pt-none sb">            About us
      </h2>
    
          <p class="c-body-2 accordion-teaser">            MSC is a large department and has a diverse staff group with a highly international composition. We thrive and find inspiration in our diversity. Our working environment is important to us and we always seek to ensure a collegial and supporting atmosphere in our daily interaction.
      </p>
    
    <div class="accordion-sub-components row row-auto row-gap-xlarge">
            
        <div class="inner-sub-components">
          


  
<article data-component-id="campus:accordionContent" class="component container accordion-content accordion-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <details class="accordion-item">
    <summary>
      <h3 class="c-paragraph-2 sb accordion-title" data-urlhash="20005-our-msc-environment">
                    Our MSC en&shy;vir&shy;on&shy;ment
      
      </h3>
    </summary>
      <div class="items">
                    


<article  data-component-id="campus:bodyText" class="component row text-component component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div class="grid-two-thirds center text-col body-headings">            <p>MSC brings together more than 100 full-time staff engaged in cutting-edge research and teaching, alongside around 100 part-time lecturers who enrich the classroom with valuable professional experience. A dynamic PhD community continuously feed into the research environment, as new projects begin and graduates move on to new opportunities. Our 12 dedicated administrative staff ensure smooth daily operations and provide essential support to both faculty and students.&nbsp;</p>
<p>MSC has a vibrant research and teaching environment and is characterized by collaboration across CBS as well as nationally and internationally, with visiting researchers from all over the world regularly hosted by the department. Externally funded projects enhance the exploration and dissemination of new research ideas, and collaboration with external stakeholders ensures channels for its impact in society.&nbsp;</p>

      </div>
</article>


      
      </div>
  </details>
</article>




  
<article data-component-id="campus:accordionContent" class="component container accordion-content accordion-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <details class="accordion-item">
    <summary>
      <h3 class="c-paragraph-2 sb accordion-title" data-urlhash="1932-msc-leadership-">
                    MSC Lead&shy;er&shy;ship 
      
      </h3>
    </summary>
      <div class="items">
                    


<article  data-component-id="campus:bodyText" class="component row text-component component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div class="grid-two-thirds center text-col body-headings">            <p>MSC’s Leadership Group as per 1 December 2025 consists of&nbsp;</p>
<ul>
<li>Head of Department Dorte Salskov-Iversen</li>
<li>Head of Secretariat Annika Dilling</li>
<li>Vice Head of Department Professor Hans Krause Hansen</li>
<li>VIP Teaching Coordinator Associate Professor Rasmus Koss Hartmann</li>
<li>External Funding Coordinator Professor Janine Leschke</li>
<li>PhD Coordinator Associate Professor Thilde Langevang&nbsp;</li>
</ul>
<p>The Leadership Group regularly consults with the MSC Department Forum which consists of representatives of the various position categories in the department.</p>

      </div>
</article>


      
      </div>
  </details>
</article>




  
<article data-component-id="campus:accordionContent" class="component container accordion-content accordion-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <details class="accordion-item">
    <summary>
      <h3 class="c-paragraph-2 sb accordion-title" data-urlhash="1934-our-history">
                    Our his&shy;tory
      
      </h3>
    </summary>
      <div class="items">
                    


<article  data-component-id="campus:bodyText" class="component row text-component component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div class="grid-two-thirds center text-col body-headings">            <p>Today’s Department of Management, Society and Communication is the result of the merger, in 2017, of the then Department of Intercultural Communication and Management and the Department of International Business Communication.&nbsp;</p>
<p>Both these departments had, in very different ways, roots in CBS’ early efforts in the 1980s and 1990s at embracing interdisciplinarity, establishing environments that were tasked with adopting a more problem oriented approach to research and teaching, leveraging the potential of bringing on board perspectives from the broader social sciences and humanities to explore business in society.&nbsp;</p>

      </div>
</article>


      
      </div>
  </details>
</article>


        </div>
      
        <div class="inner-sub-components">
          


  
<article data-component-id="campus:accordionContent" class="component container accordion-content accordion-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <details class="accordion-item">
    <summary>
      <h3 class="c-paragraph-2 sb accordion-title" data-urlhash="1930-academic-organisation-and-approach-">
                    Aca&shy;dem&shy;ic or&shy;gan&shy;isa&shy;tion and ap&shy;proach 
      
      </h3>
    </summary>
      <div class="items">
                    


<article  data-component-id="campus:bodyText" class="component row text-component component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div class="grid-two-thirds center text-col body-headings">            <p>Multi- and interdisciplinary by design, our research and teaching are informed by management and business studies as well as the broader social sciences and humanities.&nbsp;</p>
<p>Our researchers are affiliated with at least one of four research environments (two centres and two clusters with a number of subgroups). The environments constitute the primary internal anchorages for our researchers and their projects, addressing responsible management of business in society:&nbsp;</p>

      </div>
</article>


          




<article data-component-id="campus:image" class="row component container component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div data-component-id="campus:image" class="grid-two-thirds center">
                
                <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/accordion_image/public/2025-10/MSC%20organisation_september%202025.png?itok=8aqG34_E" width="900" height="652" alt="" />



      

  
      
  </div>
</article>


      
      </div>
  </details>
</article>




  
<article data-component-id="campus:accordionContent" class="component container accordion-content accordion-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <details class="accordion-item">
    <summary>
      <h3 class="c-paragraph-2 sb accordion-title" data-urlhash="1928-msc-strategy">
                    MSC strategy
      
      </h3>
    </summary>
      <div class="items">
                    


<article  data-component-id="campus:bodyText" class="component row text-component component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div class="grid-two-thirds center text-col body-headings">            <p>It is MSC’s mission to generate novel and impactful research on the dynamic relations between business and society and the responsible management thereof.&nbsp;</p>
<p>Moving forward, we aim to further develop and demonstrate the (trans)formative potential of operating in precisely this space, being the one department that explores it, research and teaching wise, at the interface between sustainability, digitalisation, inequality and global developments.&nbsp;</p>

      </div>
</article>


      
      </div>
  </details>
</article>




  
<article data-component-id="campus:accordionContent" class="component container accordion-content accordion-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <details class="accordion-item">
    <summary>
      <h3 class="c-paragraph-2 sb accordion-title" data-urlhash="20007-information-for-part-time-teachers">
                    In&shy;form&shy;a&shy;tion for part-time teach&shy;ers
      
      </h3>
    </summary>
      <div class="items">
                    


<article  data-component-id="campus:bodyText" class="component row text-component component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div class="grid-two-thirds center text-col body-headings">            <p><a href="/en/about-cbs/job-and-career/external-teacher" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="ce2f5463-ad9d-4231-aa0d-33469a4c8d2f" data-entity-substitution="canonical">Information for external lecturers can be found by following this link.</a></p>

      </div>
</article>


      
      </div>
  </details>
</article>


        </div>
          </div>
  </div>
</article>


          




  
  
  
<article data-component-id="campus:contentList" class="contentList colorprofile-component component container global-colorprofile-corporate">
  <div  data-component-id="campus:contentList" class="colorprofile dark-variant">
    <div  data-component-id="campus:contentList" class="contentList__grid-container container center">

                  <div  data-component-id="campus:contentList" class="color-primary-corporate-white display-heading">
                        <h2 id="6091-collaboration-and-outreach" class="inherit-text-color">            Col&shy;lab&shy;or&shy;a&shy;tion and out&shy;reach
      </h2>
          </div>
        
        <div class="manchet-text">
          
                  </div>

      <div class="row contentList-sub-component row-gap-large">
                    
  
<article data-component-id="campus:contentListSub" class="component grid-third image-content-list-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
      <div class="image">
                  
                <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/content_default_square/public/2025-06/BM0312_CBS-0739_mm%5B1%5D.jpg?h=d5d68e60&amp;itok=CTzxE13I" width="750" height="750" alt="" />



      

  
      
    </div>
  
  <h3 class="c-title-4 sb">
                MSC col­lab­or­a­tion 
      
  </h3>

  <div class="text">
                <p>MSC faculty are en­gaged in multiple forms of na­tion­al and in­ter­na­tion­al col­lab­or­a­tion with institutions and organisations, the work of which has a strong focus on pushing societal agendas and impact.</p>

      
  </div>

  
</article>


          
  
<article data-component-id="campus:contentListSub" class="component grid-third image-content-list-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
      <div class="image">
                  
                <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/content_default_square/public/2025-03/2024_10_23_CBS_Undervisere_Forskere_0177_KK.JPG?h=7632d73d&amp;itok=QHLrYEyf" width="750" height="750" alt="" />



      

  
      
    </div>
  
  <h3 class="c-title-4 sb">
                MSC teach&shy;ing cases 
      
  </h3>

  <div class="text">
                <p>MSC faculty regularly publish impactful teaching cases that reach students from around the world.</p>

      
  </div>

  
</article>


          
  
<article data-component-id="campus:contentListSub" class="component grid-third image-content-list-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
      <div class="image">
                  
                <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/content_default_square/public/2025-06/BM0312_CBS-0722_mm%5B1%5D.jpg?h=63bf57a8&amp;itok=WClKiacQ" width="750" height="750" alt="" />



      

  
      
    </div>
  
  <h3 class="c-title-4 sb">
                MSC ser&shy;vice to the wider so&shy;ci&shy;ety 
      
  </h3>

  <div class="text">
                <p>MSC faculty are regularly recruited to serve on boards, com­mit­tees, and oth­er bod­ies, offering advice and research-based expertise.</p>

      
  </div>

  
</article>


      
      </div>

    </div>
  </div>
</article>

        <h2 class="visually-hidden">Research and publications (Panel content)</h2>

        

  <div  class="component components_spacing tab-content component--view-mode--subsection">
                


  
<article  data-component-id="campus:twoColumnText" class="component container two-col-text-component component--view-mode--default">
  <div  data-component-id="campus:twoColumnText" class="paper-page">

                <h2 id="1937-our-research" class="two-col-header pt-none c-title-2 sb mb">            Our re&shy;search
      </h2>
    
        
<div  class="text_wrapper row row-auto row-gap-xlarge">
                <div class="body-headings"><p>Our research critically explores the roles of business and societal actors in addressing global challenges. We examine how companies and other organisations engage with issues such as sustainability, digitalization, inequality, and global development - investigating practices, responsibilities, constraints, and opportunities to create outcomes of mutual benefit.&nbsp;</p>
</div>
          <div class="body-headings"><p>Our work challenges conventional business thinking by interrogating power relations, culture, communication, and sustainability practices across diverse global and local contexts. Conceptually informed and empirically grounded, we apply both qualitative and quantitative methods. Our interdisciplinary research is collaborative in nature, spanning CBS and international academic networks.&nbsp;</p>
</div>
      
  </div>
  </div>
</article>

<article data-component-id="campus:contentListSub" class="component grid-third content-list-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  
  <h3 class="c-title-4 sb">
                The Centre for Busi&shy;ness and De&shy;vel&shy;op&shy;ment Stud&shy;ies (CBDS) 
      
  </h3>

  <div class="text">
                <p>Investigates challenges arising from the shift toward business- and market-led development, focusing on strategic changes linked to developing countries’ integration into the global economy, the geopolitics of this integration, and the move toward private sector–driven development and international partnerships.</p>

      
  </div>

      

  <a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/centre-business-and"   data-component-id="campus:button" class="btn white-arrow btn-text">
        Read more about CBDS here 
  </a>

  
</article>


          
  
<article data-component-id="campus:contentListSub" class="component grid-third content-list-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  
  <h3 class="c-title-4 sb">
                The Com&shy;mu&shy;nic&shy;a&shy;tion, Or&shy;gan&shy;isa&shy;tion and Gov&shy;ernance (COG) cluster 
      
  </h3>

  <div class="text">
                <p>Examines organizational dynamics, management, governance, and communication practices in private, public and civil society organisations, focusing on how these intersect within diverse social, cultural, economic, and political forces and contexts.</p>

      
  </div>

      

  <a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/communication-organisation-and"   data-component-id="campus:button" class="btn white-arrow btn-text">
        Read more about the COG cluster here 
  </a>

  
</article>


          
  
<article data-component-id="campus:contentListSub" class="component grid-third content-list-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  
  <h3 class="c-title-4 sb">
                The Centre for Sustain&shy;ability 
      
  </h3>

  <div class="text">
                <p>Critically explores business-in-society issues integrating the sustainability perspective into business and governance theory, practice, and the CBS curriculum. Working across disciplinary boundaries, the Centre provides an innovative space for dialogue and reflection between academia and business, both in Denmark and internationally.</p>

      
  </div>

      

  <a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/centre-sustainability"   data-component-id="campus:button" class="btn white-arrow btn-text">
        Read more about the Centre for Sustainability here 
  </a>

  
</article>


          
  
<article data-component-id="campus:contentListSub" class="component grid-third content-list-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  
  <h3 class="c-title-4 sb">
                The Tech&shy;no&shy;logy &amp; So&shy;ci&shy;ety cluster 
      
  </h3>

  <div class="text">
                <p>Explores technologies and digital transformations, focusing on their implications for organizational and societal development. It particularly addresses how emerging technologies interact with both new and established organizations, markets, and society in local and global contexts.</p>

<article  data-component-id="campus:accordion" class="component container accordion-component component--view-mode--default">
  <div class="paper-page">
                <h2 id="1951-externally-funded-projects" class="c-title-2 pt-none sb">            Ex&shy;tern&shy;ally fun&shy;ded pro&shy;jects
      </h2>
    
          <p class="c-body-2 accordion-teaser">            MSC has a large portfolio of externally funded research projects. Below, you will find a selection of recently granted projects.
      </p>
    
    <div class="accordion-sub-components row row-auto row-gap-xlarge">
            
        <div class="inner-sub-components">
          


  
<article data-component-id="campus:accordionContent" class="component container accordion-content accordion-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <details class="accordion-item">
    <summary>
      <h3 class="c-paragraph-2 sb accordion-title" data-urlhash="53543-market-power-climate-change-and-vulnerability-in-tanzanias-food-value-chains-mapocavu">
                    Mar&shy;ket Power, Cli&shy;mate Change and Vul&shy;ner&shy;ab&shy;il&shy;ity in Tan&shy;zania&#039;s Food Value Chains (MA&shy;PO&shy;CAVU)
      
      </h3>
    </summary>
      <div class="items">
                    


<article  data-component-id="campus:bodyText" class="component row text-component component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div class="grid-two-thirds center text-col body-headings">            <p><strong>The project:</strong><br>The Market Power, Climate Change and Vulnerability in Tanzania's Food Value Chains (MAPOCAVU) project is a collaborative research initiative involving Copenhagen Business School (Denmark), Sokoine University of Agriculture and the University of Dodoma (Tanzania), and the University of Johannesburg (South Africa). The project examines how market power and climate change interact to shape vulnerability among smallholder farmers and low-income food consumers in Tanzania.</p>
<p>Focusing on the value chains of the country's two most important staple grains, maize and rice, MAPOCAVU traces the journey of these commodities from production and trade to processing, retail, and consumption. The project challenges two common assumptions in development interventions: first, that food markets are primarily composed of small competing traders; and second, that climate adaptation should be addressed mainly through local and national interventions.</p>
<p>Through an interdisciplinary approach combining climate science, political economy, value chain analysis, competition economics, and food security research, MAPOCAVU investigates how market power affects resilience and vulnerability along food value chains. The project generates evidence to inform food, climate, and competition policies at local, national, and regional levels, with the aim of reducing vulnerability among disadvantaged producers and consumers.</p>
<p>MAPOCAVU also contributes to long-term research capacity building through collaborative North-South and South-South partnerships, including the training of three PhD candidates and extensive engagement with government agencies, businesses, civil society organisations, and local communities.</p>
<p><strong>Collaborative partners:</strong></p>
<ol type="1">
<li>Sokoine University of Agriculture (SUA), Tanzania</li>
<li>University of Dodoma (UDOM), Tanzania</li>
<li>University of Johannesburg (UJ), South Africa</li>
<li>Copenhagen Business School (CBS), Denmark</li>
</ol>
<p><strong>Project team:</strong></p>
<ol>
<li>Abiud Bongole, Innocensia Yanghura Pato, Jacqueline Majuva (PhD Candidate), Judith Valerian, Reuben Kadigi, Roselyne Alphonse, Win Luhwago (PhD Candidate) from SUA</li>
<li>Mathew Bukhi Mabele, Ng’winamila Kasongi, Barnabas Msongaleli, Wilhelm Kiwango, and Dismas Alex (PhD Candidate) from UDOM</li>
<li>Grace Nsomba and Arthur Mahuma from UJ</li>
<li><a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/stefano-ponte" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="3267f467-f8f4-483b-a39c-cab9be10b7ab" data-entity-substitution="canonical">Stefano Ponte </a>(Principal Investigator),<a href="/en/research/departments/department-international-economics-government-and-business-egb/mogens-kamp" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="3e14e801-7368-4452-9aa1-253fda3964ef" data-entity-substitution="canonical"> Mogens Kamp Justesen,</a><a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/lisa-ann-richey" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="f00ce81f-031c-4b08-9081-cf3415afaa4e" data-entity-substitution="canonical"> Lisa Ann Richey,</a> and Lars Thomsen from the Department of Management, Society and Communication (MSC), CBS</li>
</ol>
<p><strong>The grant:&nbsp;</strong><br>Funded by the Danida Fellowship Centre (DFC) under the Research Collaboration Projects administered by the Consultative Research Committee for Development Research (FFU). Project period: 2026–2030.</p>
<p><strong>For more information, please contact:</strong><br><a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/stefano-ponte" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="3267f467-f8f4-483b-a39c-cab9be10b7ab" data-entity-substitution="canonical">Stefano Ponte </a>(Principal Investigator), Copenhagen Business School | <a href="mailto:spo.msc@cbs.dk">spo.msc@cbs.dk</a></p>
<p>&nbsp;</p>

      </div>
</article>


      
      </div>
  </details>
</article>




  
<article data-component-id="campus:accordionContent" class="component container accordion-content accordion-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <details class="accordion-item">
    <summary>
      <h3 class="c-paragraph-2 sb accordion-title" data-urlhash="53026-ripple---unsustainable-tipping-points">
                    Ripple - (Un)sus&shy;tain&shy;able Tip&shy;ping Points
      
      </h3>
    </summary>
      <div class="items">
                    


<article  data-component-id="campus:bodyText" class="component row text-component component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div class="grid-two-thirds center text-col body-headings">            <p><strong>The project:</strong><br>How do sustainable behaviours spread - and when do they trigger resistance rather than wider adoption?</p>
<p><strong>(Un)sustainable Tipping Points (Ripple)</strong> investigates the social mechanisms that can turn individual green choices into large-scale behavioural change. The project focuses on social contagion and behavioural thresholds: how people respond to others’ actions, how many adopters are needed before behaviour shifts, and when small changes develop into self-reinforcing social tipping points.&nbsp;</p>
<p>The project also examines the counter-dynamics that can slow, reverse or hinder sustainable change, including resistance associated with values and identity, moralization and perceived social norms. Using large-scale behavioural data from digital platforms alongside experiments and surveys, the project studies sustainable choices.&nbsp;</p>
<p>By integrating insights from social psychology, behavioural economics and sustainability science, the project aims to develop empirically grounded models of social tipping dynamics and identify when sustainable behaviours spread - or fail to spread. The findings will provide insights relevant to climate action and policies seeking to accelerate durable collective behavioural change.</p>
<p><strong>Project team:</strong><br><a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/kristian-roed-nielsen" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="7b60c142-d2ec-485c-8d08-e36dbe9840e3" data-entity-substitution="canonical"><strong>Kristian Roed Nielsen</strong></a>, Associate Professor, Department of Management, Society and Communication, Copenhagen Business School</p>
<p><a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/alice-pizzo" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="21e03f89-17e0-4cf6-a7d3-6c5c8f36108d" data-entity-substitution="canonical"><strong>Alice Pizzo</strong></a>, Assistant Professor, Department of Management, Society and Communication, Copenhagen Business School.&nbsp;</p>
<p><strong>The grant:</strong><br>The project is funded by the Independent Research Fund Denmark (DFF) under the thematic programme Green Research. The grant amounts to DKK 6,868,065 and runs from 2025 to 2030.</p>
<p><strong>For more information</strong>, please contact<strong> </strong><a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/kristian-roed-nielsen" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="7b60c142-d2ec-485c-8d08-e36dbe9840e3" data-entity-substitution="canonical"><strong>Kristian Roed Nielsen</strong></a>, Associate Professor, Department of Management, Society and Communication, Copenhagen Business School, <a href="mailto:krn.msc@cbs.dk">krn.msc@cbs.dk</a>&nbsp;</p>
<p>&nbsp;</p>

      </div>
</article>


      
      </div>
  </details>
</article>




  
<article data-component-id="campus:accordionContent" class="component container accordion-content accordion-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <details class="accordion-item">
    <summary>
      <h3 class="c-paragraph-2 sb accordion-title" data-urlhash="53028--africa-business-and-society-abs">
                     Africa, Busi&shy;ness and So&shy;ci&shy;ety (ABS)
      
      </h3>
    </summary>
      <div class="items">
                    


<article  data-component-id="campus:bodyText" class="component row text-component component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div class="grid-two-thirds center text-col body-headings">            <p><strong>The project:</strong><br>The Africa, Business and Society (ABS) programme is a partnership between Copenhagen Business School (CBS), Denmark, and three leading African universities: the University of Ghana Business School, the University of Nairobi (Kenya), and the University of Johannesburg (South Africa). The project aims to transform how business education engages with the Global South by integrating African perspectives into business curricula, strengthening South-South and North-South academic collaboration, and promoting research-based teaching. The programme addresses the gap between dominant business theories developed in the Global North and the realities of African economies, where businesses play a key role in innovation, digitalization, renewable energy, and social development. Through faculty exchanges, joint teaching, curriculum development, student mobility, scholarships, and collaborative research, ABS seeks to educate a new generation of ethical entrepreneurs, critical thinkers, and responsible leaders. The programme also supports Master’s students from partner universities to study in the Business and the Global South MSc programme at CBS, fostering intercultural learning and long-term institutional partnerships.</p>
<p><strong>Project team:</strong><br>Department of Management, Society and Communication (MSC), Copenhagen Business School. Core CBS team includes<a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/lindsay-whitfield" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="2fff057b-694b-456d-8750-2f9b4fa585ab" data-entity-substitution="canonical"> Lindsay Whitfield</a>, <a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/lisa-ann-richey" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="f00ce81f-031c-4b08-9081-cf3415afaa4e" data-entity-substitution="canonical">Lisa Ann Richey</a>, <a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/stefano-ponte" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="3267f467-f8f4-483b-a39c-cab9be10b7ab" data-entity-substitution="canonical">Stefano Ponte</a>, <a href="/en/research/departments/department-international-economics-government-and-business-egb/mogens-kamp" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="3e14e801-7368-4452-9aa1-253fda3964ef" data-entity-substitution="canonical">Mogens Kamp Justesen</a>,<a href="/en/research/departments/department-organization/stine-haakonsson" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="78004df5-bbca-49eb-b621-536bae741186" data-entity-substitution="canonical"> Stine Haakonsson</a>,<a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/thilde-langevang" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="f8f24231-9e57-425d-b6fd-47e72a85e268" data-entity-substitution="canonical"> Thilde Langevang</a>, and <a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/soren-jeppesen" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="543727b8-90a6-4851-ac5b-3d36f7307291" data-entity-substitution="canonical">Søren Jeppesen</a></p>
<p><strong>Collaborative partners:</strong><br>University of Ghana Business School (Ghana), University of Nairobi (Kenya), University of Johannesburg (South Africa)</p>
<p><strong>The grant:&nbsp;</strong><br>Funded by the Danida Fellowship Centre (DFC) under the Knowledge and Innovation Programme (KIP). Project period: 2026–2030 (1 January 2026 to 31 December 2030).</p>
<p><strong>For more information</strong>, please contact <a href="/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/lindsay-whitfield" data-entity-type="node" data-entity-uuid="2fff057b-694b-456d-8750-2f9b4fa585ab" data-entity-substitution="canonical">Professor Lindsay Whitfield</a> (Project Lead) <a href="mailto:lwh.msc@cbs.dk">lwh.msc@cbs.dk</a></p>

      </div>
</article>


      
      </div>
  </details>
</article>




  
<article data-component-id="campus:accordionContent" class="component container accordion-content accordion-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <details class="accordion-item">
    <summary>
      <h3 class="c-paragraph-2 sb accordion-title" data-urlhash="53360-d-core-dark-side-of-corporate-responsibility">
                    D-CORE: Dark Side of Cor&shy;por&shy;ate Respons&shy;ibility
      
      </h3>
    </summary>
      <div class="items">
                    


<article  data-component-id="campus:bodyText" class="component row text-component component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div class="grid-two-thirds center text-col body-headings">            <p><strong>The project:</strong> DCORE investigates content moderation work, the different forms it takes, and the mechanisms through which it affects human workers. Content moderation is essential to the social responsibility of digital platforms: moderators (human and human-trained AI models) remove harmful, violent, abusive, and otherwise objectionable material to make online environments safer. Yet this responsibility creates significant negative spillovers for those tasked with performing it. In particular, content moderation exposes workers to a distinctive form of emotional and psychological work generated through sustained engagement with disturbing digital content.<br><br>Conceptualizing this phenomenon as a governance challenge at the dark side of corporate social responsibility, DCORE asks how organizations can fulfil their responsibilities toward platform users without transferring disproportionate costs to workers operating behind the interface. Empirically, the study will draw on in-depth interviews with content moderators and governance actors within platform companies around the world.</p>
<p><strong>Project team:</strong> MSC:&nbsp;<br><a href="https://www.cbs.dk/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/tanusree-jain">Tanusree Jain</a>, PI<br><a href="https://www.cbs.dk/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/dan-karreman">Dan Karreman</a>, Team Member&nbsp;<br><a href="https://www.cbs.dk/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/gianlorenzo-meggio">Gianlorenzo Meggio</a>, Post Doctoral Fellow<br><a href="https://www.cbs.dk/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/archana-raghavan">Archana Raghavan</a>, Post Doctoral Fellow<br><a href="https://www.cbs.dk/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/noa-celestini">Noa Celestini</a>, Research Assistant&nbsp;</p>
<p><strong>The grant:</strong> Carlsberg: Semper Ardens: Accelerate<br>2026 - 2029</p>
<p><strong>For more information, </strong>please contact PI Professor <a href="https://www.cbs.dk/en/research/departments/department-management-society-and-communication/tanusree-jain">Tanusree Jain</a>, tja.msc@cbs.d</p>

      </div>
</article>


      
      </div>
  </details>
</article>




  
<article data-component-id="campus:accordionContent" class="component container accordion-content accordion-sub-component component--view-mode--default">
  <details class="accordion-item">
    <summary>
      <h3 class="c-paragraph-2 sb accordion-title" data-urlhash="52324-beloop-‘from-awareness-to-action-evidence-based-pathways-to-accelerate-europe’s-transition-to-a-circular-consumption’">
                    BE&shy;LOOP ‘From aware&shy;ness to ac&shy;tion: evid&shy;ence-based path&shy;ways to ac&shy;cel&shy;er&shy;ate Europe’s trans&shy;ition to a cir&shy;cu&shy;lar con&shy;sump&shy;tion’
      
      </h3>
    </summary>
      <div class="items">
                    


<article  data-component-id="campus:bodyText" class="component row text-component component--view-mode--accordion">
  <div class="grid-two-thirds center text-col body-headings">            <p>Across Europe, most consumption patterns remain linear, with limited evidence on how to shift behaviours toward a circular economy. BELOOP tackles this challenge by combining social science, technology, and real-world pilots to turn awareness into practical action. The project begins by mapping barriers, attitudes, misconceptions, and motivations shaping consumer behaviour and readiness for change. Building on these insights, consumers, economic actors, and researchers co-create interventions, supported by actionable evidence from Life Cycle Sustainability Assessment (LCSA). An AI-powered tool forecasts the impact of proposed measures, ensuring only the most promising strategies move to real-world testing. These are evaluated through experimental and quasi-experimental designs across four high-impact domains: electronics, packaging, mobility and textiles. Those results are translated into replication toolkits, training programmes, and governance playbooks aligned with EU policies. BELOOP delivers governance, policy, and business pathways that empower consumers, civil society organisations, public authorities and businesses to drive Europe’s transition to a circular economy. Supported by a comprehensive communication, engagement, and replication strategy, operationalised through dedicated hubs, BELOOP maximises impact and ensures EU-wide uptake of successful solutions, enabling large-scale and lasting adoption.</p>
<p><strong>Project team:&nbsp;</strong>MSC</p>
<p><strong>Collaborative partners:&nbsp;</strong></p>
<ol>
<li>DRAXIS RESEARCH VENTURES ASTIKI MI KERDOSKOPIKI ET... (Coordinator) – Greece&nbsp;</li>
<li>Copenhagen Business School – Denmark&nbsp;</li>
